# Switches

Every setting that turns a behaviour on in `lamp-voice`, `lamp-body`, `lamp-eyes`,
`lamp-expression`, `lamp-name`, `lamp-skills`, `lamp-actions`, `lamp-tasks`, `lamp-controls`, `lamp-ears`, `lamp-cloud` and `lamp-mood`: what it does,
what lamp-4ace and the product set, the pull request that added it with the lamp
check it named, and the product default proposed for it. Made for the owner's
question of 17 October, "why off?", and for switching lamp-4ace on group by
group: each switch that passes its check becomes the default in code before
1 November (the first builder, #705, 07:30).

**As of** `lamp-voice` `8c3df0449` (17 October 2026, about 08:50 +07), and the
product column as #1332 (17 October, 15:41) sets it, with `LAMP_SPOKEN_TO`
from #1378. The code is the source of
truth; this page is a census of it, to be kept up as switches are added, turned
on or removed.

## How to read it

- **Values and default:** what the code accepts, and what happens when the
  setting is absent. Almost every switch is off unless set.
- **lamp-4ace (repo unit)** and **Product unit:** what
  `lampOS/deploy/lamp-4ace/` and `lampOS/deploy/product/` set, reading only
  lines that aren't commented out. lamp-4ace may also run drop-ins that aren't
  in the repo (see "What the census found"). A switch that is on by its
  code's default, with no unit setting it, is written exactly `on (unset)`.
  That is the one cell the stable notes read, since no file states that
  default: such a switch is never listed as built but not on.
- **Added by:** the merged pull request that brought the name in, or the commit
  for the ones added to `lamp-voice` directly before pull requests were the rule.
- **Lamp check it named:** what that pull request or its doc said must be seen
  on a lamp, and whether a doc records it **shown**. Where nothing is recorded,
  it says so; no lamp result here is assumed.
- **Proposed product default:** "on (already)" where both units already set it;
  "on" where the evidence is in and it should be switched on; "off until …"
  naming the check that would turn it on; "off" for a diagnostic or a switch
  turned down.
- **Counted as:** `on`, `now`, `until`, `off` or `setting`, for the counts
  (see "The count").
- Paths, sockets and test hooks (`LAMP_*_SOCKET`, `LAMP_*_DIR`, `LAMP_*_FILE`,
  `LAMP_*_TEST_*`, `LAMP_BUS`, `LAMP_BUS_ROOT`, `LAMP_CLOCK`) and tuning numbers
  (speeds, poses, reaches) are left out: they configure, they don't turn a
  behaviour on.

The generic voice template (`deploy/units/lamp-voice.service`) also sets
`LAMP_TOOLS`, `LAMP_CLOCK`, `LAMP_TIME_PLACES`, `LAMP_KERNEL_CLOCK` and
`LAMP_SESSION_FACT` to `on`, matching the product. The update unit census
checks all five, and its function test reads the generic unit to check the
clock declaration, time context and session fact. They need the system
clock and zone files, not product-only configuration. A clock the kernel
does not know to be synchronised is reported as unset; the local zone
still needs to be correct. This is configuration and host-test evidence,
not a moved-lamp spoken-time or latency result.

## Reserved repair controller name

`LAMP_FACE_REPAIR` is reserved by the inactive face-repair controller core.
The core does not read it: setting it in the environment enables no runtime,
RPC or product capability. Its constructor takes an explicit boolean for host
controls. Neither product nor lamp units set it. It is outside the behavior
counts until the separately reviewed runtime integration implements the switch.

## The count

The counts aren't kept on this page: a test works them out from the rows, so
that two pull requests that each add a switch never touch the same line (the
first builder, #705, 18 October 07:28). Each row's last column, **Counted as**,
says how the switch is counted:

| Counted as | Means |
|---|---|
| `on` | On already, or right at its default |
| `now` | Proposed to turn on now |
| `until` | Off until its named lamp check |
| `off` | Stays off (diagnostic, turned down, or not needed) |
| `setting` | A setting, not a behaviour |

To see the counts, by table and in all:

```sh
cargo test -p lamp-voice-eval --test it switches:: -- --nocapture
```

`lamp-voice-eval`'s `tests/it/switches.rs` fails if a row in the four tables
below has no **Counted as**, or one that isn't one of the five.

**Proposed to turn on now**, with its newest lamp-4ace record a pass:
- `LAMP_LOOK_FULL=on` **with** `lamp-eyes --full-look`, the two together: it
  works end to end on lamp-4ace and has answered there since 16 Oct 03:17
  (#842), with things held up by a person shown on lamp-52e6.

`LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` and `LAMP_TASKS_WHO` were proposed here at first
on earlier passes; each has a newer lamp-4ace record that fails (the reviewer
on #1238), so each is "off until" its scenes pass. So was `LAMP_SPOKEN_TO`,
until the battery of 17 October showed the product deaf in a busy room
without it (#828, 19:22): it is on in the product unit since, and its two
failing scenes are for Sunday's two-people table (#829).

**The quickest wins among "off until"** are the switches whose check needs no
motion, since lamp-4ace's motors stay still until Monday: `LAMP_STAFF_NOTE`
(the in-time/late count), `LAMP_SHORT_SOUNDS=quiet-room-checked`,
`LAMP_ASKER_FIRST=record`, `LAMP_STOP_TOLD`, `LAMP_WAIT_KEEPS_ANSWER`,
`LAMP_TASK_NEWS_AT_PAUSE`, `lamp-eyes --leave-still-faces` (one live night),
`lamp-expression --offline-face`, `lamp-eyes --look-on-change` (one night) and
`lamp-name --quiet-below` (an hour's levels, then a night).

| LAMP_CUT_IN_COUNTS_REAL | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex`. A resume counts toward the two fruitless yields (`FRUITLESS_YIELDS`) only when its yield's evidence window points at the lamp's own voice: from the onset run through the settling block (at most 120 blocks), `Own` and not-plain `Over` blocks outnumber plain `Over` and `Clear`; `Unknown`, `Under`, `Still` and no evidence count for neither, and a tie does not count (`YieldEvidence`). Any other resume counts on its own bound instead: three (`OTHER_FRUITLESS_YIELDS`) fall back for the reply as two of the lamp's own voice do; the 20 s gives one back to each, a commit clears both, and with `LAMP_CUT_IN_KEEPS_LISTENING` each allows two more. So a sound that is never a line pauses a story at most three times in 20 s (five with keeps-listening). The two counts are separate, not summed: mixed with the lamp's own voice they allow at most four pauses (one own, two others, then one more that falls back whichever count it adds to), eight with keeps-listening (three own, four others, one more). | LAMP_BACKCHANNEL_HOLDS | `on` only; off; needs `--turn-taking duplex` and `LAMP_STOP_WORDS=heard` or `heard-always`, inert without either (one journal line says so); with `heard-always` the reply's one stream tells the yield its words, only its own final results, interim ones and those ending before the yield passed over, a backchannel told again as words if a later final adds more (#2939, 23 Oct; reviewer-two's P2 on #3200). While a yield's words are awaited its commit is held for them: on the quiet after a plain word until they come, on speech after the lamp's sound only while that speech is under 700 ms (`BACKCHANNEL_SPEECH_BLOCKS`; 400 ms until lamp-52e6 on 23 Oct, where a played "Yeah." and "Uh-huh." reached it and committed); no hold past 500 ms after the words are asked for (`BACKCHANNEL_WORDS_WAIT_BLOCKS`, after 100 ms of stillness) or past the yield's 1.1 s deadline. Words of one or two, all backchannels (`stop_words::backchannel`, read after hyphens and other dashes are joined: mm, mmm, mhm, mhmm, mmhm, mmhmm, uhhuh, uhhum, yeah, yea, yeh, yah, yes, yep, yup, ok, okay, okey, alright, right, sure; oh, ah, hm and hmm only before another (`LEADS_ONLY`: "Oh, okay." is one, a lone "Oh." or "Hmm." commits); a pair "I see", "all right", "uh huh", "uh hum", "mm hm", "mm hmm" or "got it" counting as one word; uh, uh-uh, so, no, what and huh left out; Vietnamese with `stt_language` vi: ừ, ừm, ờ, à, ồ, dạ, vâng, ok; not "rồi" or "đúng"), no "?", no stop phrase, and the lamp's last finished sentence before the yield not a question (a "Yes." to "Shall I go on?" is an answer), let the resume wait resume the answer and no turn starts; a backchannel commits only once its speech reaches 700 ms. Anything else commits at once; words that come empty or only punctuation ("...") are no words; no words by the bound, or before the yield ends, resume the answer as a backchannel does (room sound or the lamp's own echo, lamp-52e6 on #3200), and the journal says the hold ran out; only speech reaching 700 ms commits without words, or speech going on 60 ms in the clear after the words were asked for (the ask heard only what came before its pause: lamp-52e6, 24 Oct, "Wait", a 300 ms pause, then a question, came back as no words and was held to the deadline); words that come later are dropped with the yield. A stop phrase, "wait" and a forced `stop/heard` are never held. The second route only, with `LAMP_ATTENTION` and `LAMP_NAME_OR_FOLLOW_UP`: in a busy room, a line begun within 20 s (`SAME_SPEAKER_CARRIES_FOR`) of the end of the lamp's last words, by its start, past a follow-up's 8 s or after talk past the lamp, goes on as the conversation when it is the one the lamp answered: the caller's face alone seen speaking it turned to the lamp (the eyes' speaking faces), or the conversation's voice at `SAME_FROM` (0.70) or over. Another face seen speaking it, or a sure other voice (under 0.30 from 2.5 s of speech or more, `CARRY_SURE_OTHER_SPEECH_SAMPLES`, which no print reaches today), is held; one such line, or one held past a still turned face, or a print known late as a sure other voice, ends nothing, does not make the room busy by voice alone and is no talk (`RoomTold::colleague_held`), and the owner goes on (the first builder at 09:34 on #3296, replacing 03:46's one-line end); two lines in a row from someone else end the carry until the lamp answers again, as two lines left alone end a conversation; a colleague who calls the lamp by name is the caller from then on; the 20 s run from the later of the lamp's last words and the actual audio end of one held colleague line, once per actual answer, capped at 40 s from the lamp's last words (First's decision on Queue #3126, 24 Oct 12:57); delayed classification uses that recorded end, never its processing time; if it first opens the window at the current verdict, that line is rechecked by its captured start and its own positive owner/nearness evidence in Busy, Unknown and Quiet, with no wait for a missing print; a current-line likeness consumed by the first lookup is retained for that check rather than replaced by a second empty lookup (the answer clock is `Did::Said` queued-PCM bookkeeping, not an acoustic drain receipt); so do a stop and a privacy change, and a forgetting leaves no words to carry from. The caller's face seen speaking turned away is held and ends the carry, their voice sure or not; turning back needs a fresh call or answer. These signs are read in any room: in a room not busy, unknown or quiet, such a line, or one in a voice not surely the owner's, is held as in a busy room, the room unchanged. The owner's carried line is no reply to a colleague's question held before it (no `AFTER_QUESTION` hold). Voice-only identity carry must be nearer the conversation's than every held line's print by `CARRY_NEARER_BY` (0.10, provisional): up to eight per-call request/optional-print constraints, no eviction. Missing held prints require the owner's face until their own cached reply arrives; a ninth distinct held line latches face-only identity carry until a fresh call. Duplicates only fill missing evidence; owner admissions/answers do not reset constraints, but a fresh call, stop, privacy, observed forget, turn-away or two consecutive other lines clears them and fences old callbacks. An observed forgotten/absent answer closes the active carry too, so a subsequent legitimately admitted line begins a new scope even if a recent call still permits follow-up; old callbacks cannot revive it, and capacity is not reset inside a live scope. Past the ordinary follow-up, a positive current voice refused by these constraints is held in Busy, Unknown and Quiet without making the stored room busy. Generic missing/weak current-evidence personal scope in Unknown/Quiet remains a separate acceptance hold; the first 8 s follow-up is unchanged. A short print under 0.70 is no sign either way, so with short lines the face carries. A line begun within the 8 s while the carry stands is read the same way: another face seen speaking it is held, and a line with no sure sign either way follows the ordinary follow-up rule whatever the print's arrival (at the verdict, late or never), an unsure print taken for no print. In short, the first 8 s are not identity-gated; past 8 s only a sure same-speaker sign carries; always held, inside the 8 s too: another face seen speaking, the caller turned away, and a sure other voice (not reachable today: prints carry at most 1.5 s of speech, under the provisional 2.5 s). A print that was no evidence never becomes evidence: a going-on line's print joins the conversation's voice only at 0.70 or over or seen spoken by the caller's face, at its verdict or late, caller-only on or off. Each answer renews the 20 s, so a chain of turns has no overall cap, but outside the 8 s window each carried turn needs its own sure sign. Such a line the model leaves unanswered is no talk (`RoomTold::carried`) | — | — | Sixth, the owner's P1 on Queue #3126 (23 Oct) | Tests only: five turns at 12 s gaps carried by voice and face, voice alone, face alone and face with 0.27 / 0.00 short prints; There, up to 7 s of silence reads as broken (the reviewer's P3 2 on #2307), so the run checks those too before it goes on, or the wait is narrowed to news first | until |

On the second route, while a line is heard, its worker tells the coordinator whenever the words so far come to end on an unfinished word, or no longer do (`ProviderOutput::WordsEnd`, the class only, never the words): a joining word ("about", "the", "a", "and", "to", "so", "like", "but", "or", "of", "for", "with", "my", or Vietnamese "và", "của", "là", "thì", "mà", "để") or a filler ("um", "uh"), not followed by "?" or "!", compared composed (NFC); no question words, as "tell me why" is a whole request (`unfinished_words::UNFINISHED_WORDS`). The filler class works on the second route because a request's line then asks the service for its filler words (`filler_words=true`; not the stop or wait words' streams, nor Flux), which Deepgram drops by default (#3126: on lamp-4ace 44 holds were `joining`, 0 `filler`); the fillers are taken out of the line's final and early words before anything else reads it (the join, the not-for-me words, the name checks, the thread, the model). While they do, an end the detector decides (silence window or end-of-turn verdict) is held for at most 1.3 s more of quiet per line, not per pause, and never past the window plus 1.3 s (`unfinished_words::WAIT_BLOCKS`); an end Smart Turn already held as incomplete (`model_incomplete`) is not held again: speech in it goes on with the same line, and finished words told meanwhile let it end at once. How long after the end of the lamp's own words a line begins and is still in the conversation going on, in a busy room. 15 s at most is policy, matching how long a busy room keeps a conversation going on without the switch (`BUSY_GOING_ON_FOR`): with the switch, in a busy room, the window alone decides, so a longer one would let desk talk in longer. While nobody is known (the room's scope), a `remember` whose text LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_AFTER_FORGET's detector (`remember::about_someone`: first or third person words, "the user", or a possessive `'s`) marks as about a person is not kept, and the model is told `{"remembered": false}` with a note to say, short and friendly, that it wasn't kept and that they can say who they are or let the lamp see their face and say it again (no promise of a later keep); the observation pass drops such proposals. A room fact is kept, and a known face's own is kept to them. An unknown speaker's single-item forget runs on the room (older room observations may contain a guest's words); its result says what room memory was removed or that nothing matched, never claiming a personal erase. The id is never told nor picks what is said. An older memory: the line is sent again without it and kept, once journalled, and `forget_said`'s `unknown_op` removes nothing more. A line that plainly asks the lamp to keep something at a sentence's start (`remember::asked::asks_to_keep`: "remember that …", "remember my …", "please remember …", "can you remember that …", "don't forget that …", "keep in mind that …", "make a note that …") must call `remember` in its first chat round (`tool_choice`), after a named ask and a face ask, which come first. | LAMP_SCHEDULE_ASKED | `on` only; off; reaches the second route only, whose chat can be made to call a function. A line that plainly asks for a timer or a reminder at a sentence's start (`tools::schedule_asked::asks_to_set`: "remind me …", "set a timer …", "set a reminder …", "set an alarm …", "wake me …", "timer for …", after a greeting, "please" or "can/could/would/will you") must call the function that sets it in its first chat round (`tool_choice`): `set_timer` for a duration from now ("in three minutes", "for ten minutes"), `set_reminder` for a clock time ("at nine", "tomorrow at 6:30", "noon"), after a named ask, a face ask and a keep ask. The time must come right after the ask ("remind me in three minutes …", "remind me tomorrow at 6:30 …", "wake me up at seven", "a timer for ten minutes"; "for" gives a length only to a timer's ask), a clock time must stand as one ("at nine.", "at 6:30 to …", "at seven in the morning", not "at 180 degrees"), and after the time come only "to" and the task, or "please": a task's length stands ("… to exercise for twenty minutes"), but a second time or a day in the task ("… at ten", "… tomorrow"), a condition ("… when the oven is ready"), a clause after a comma ("…, translate that into French") or a time later in the line forces nothing, and a length runs from now, so "set a timer tomorrow for ten minutes" forces nothing. It errs toward not forcing: the line must be one sentence, the ask, beside at most a greeting ("Lamp."), so a second sentence ("Ignore that.", "I changed my mind.", a translation around a quote) leaves it to the model; and any word that may take the ask back, put it in doubt or make it another operation anywhere in the line ("don't", "not", "no", "wait", "hold", "cancel", "stop", "never", "forget", "bother", "actually", "or", "ignore", "skip", "scrap", "leave it", …; a repeat or a span: "every", "on Mondays", "twice", "day", "week"; a later start or window: "starting", "until"; a condition: "when", "if", "once"), quote marks (single or double), brackets, a dash or a `%`, a question that isn't an ask, and a keep taken back leave it to the model. The next round, with the call's result, is asked as before, so the lamp says what the schedule did. The start says it once | — | on | the first builder, 25 Oct | lamp-4ace on 6819's product unit, 20:07: "Lamp, remind me in three minutes to call my sister." was answered "Three minutes, call your sister." with no call, and nothing was said three minutes later | on | the lamp never says it set what it didn't | on |

The typed name leaves the model's instruction and is told with a line only when it is the owner's: the line's memory person (its scope lease, narrowed to the room once another face is seen speaking) is the known face the eyes keep under the typed name, with at most that one face in view (`humans/faces/tracked`). The second route judges every turn on that line's own lease, with or without `LAMP_SECOND_SCOPED_CONTEXT`, never on the person its session is counted for (that stays the owner's for 30 s after they go), and tells it right after the character; a turn with no lease is told none. The Live route tells it as a note on the open line, counted told only once the service answers that line (a line given up before its End never wrote it, so the next is told it); a session that may hold it is renewed fresh, between turns, as soon as whoever is at the lamp may not have it, and a line that begins in it before then is not sent (`crates/voice/src/person.rs`, `may_tell`, `live_holds`). Without a memory there is no person lease, so the Live route never tells it. Just after setup no face is kept, so the owner isn't told their name until "remember my face" under the typed name. A line said to someone else that names an agent becomes a prepared hand-over and a question to the room (the heard yes still guards the start). ## The voice's own flags (`lamp-voice serve`)

`lamp-voice`'s command line in its unit, beside its environment. Here the code's
default and the units often differ: both units already pass the lamp's choice, so
"make it the code default" is the proposal (the first builder's 07:30: a switch
that passes becomes the default in code). Missing from the first version of this
page; the Second's 07:45 on #1238.

| Switch | Values and default | lamp-4ace (repo unit) | Product unit | Added by | Lamp check it named | Proposed product default | Why (one line) | Counted as |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--turn-taking` | `vad-only` (default) / `v1-baseline` / `duplex`. `duplex`: a person heard over the lamp makes it yield | duplex | duplex | commit 7dde135af (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace in every scene since 13 Oct (duplex-listening.md) | duplex (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; `vad-only` is the experiment's first form | on |
| `--endpoint` | `silence` (default) / `smart-turn` / `smart-turn-fast`. The end-of-turn model judges each pause | smart-turn-fast | smart-turn-fast | commit 6ff31ca1d (direct; `smart-turn-fast` 86e5c275a) | endpoint-pilot.md: the fast model's verdict in 148 ms on lamp-4ace, before the silence window ends the turn | smart-turn-fast (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; without the model every pause is the window's | on |
| `--noise-suppression` | `on` (default) / `off`. The capture's software noise suppression | off | off | commit 64529dee4 (direct) | Off on lamp-4ace since the voice came in | off (already); make it the code default | Both units turn it off; the code default disagrees with every lamp | on |
| `--reference-clock` | `fixed` (default) / `capture`. The echo reference follows the two USB clocks' difference | capture | capture | commit 55d7af98e (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace since 13 Oct | capture (already); make it the code default | `fixed` is a margin a long session uses up (options.rs) | on |
| `--reference-recovery` | `off` (default) / `bounded`. Re-centres a reference whose margin was used up | off (lamp-4ace's drop-in passes `bounded` since 17 Oct morning) | bounded (#1332) | commit 55d7af98e (direct) | lamp-cb94 ran an hour clean on `bounded`, 17 Oct 07:21–08:23 UTC: no restart, no untrusted reference (Twelve, #1298); with `off` its voice ended on `ReferenceLate` at 04:53 (#1178) | bounded (#1332) | A held-up speaker no longer ends the capture worker and the voice (#1298) | on |
| `--echo-reference` | `as-given` (default) / `steady`. `steady` delays the reference to where the echo is | — | — | commit 8a494575f (direct, 14 Oct) | Played on lamp-4ace 14 Oct 18:00–19:35, six runs: with `steady` the lamp paused for nothing 35 times in 52 trials and gave up its own story 6 times, so it stays off there (steady-reference.md) | off (`as-given`) | Shown worse on the lamp; its own doc calls it an experiment, never a default | off |
| `--provider-continuation` | `off` (default) / `kept-start`. A next request's start kept back while an answer may still come | kept-start | kept-start | commit 2021c1bb9 (direct) | Runs on lamp-4ace since 13 Oct | kept-start (already); make it the code default | Both units pass it; `--early-end-ms` and the cut-off note need it | on |
| `--controls-stop` | flag; off. A hand on the button or the head stops the lamp's speech | set | set | commit 5f9914314 (direct, 14 Oct) | On lamp-4ace since 14 Oct (controls.md) | on (already) | Both units pass it; a stop by hand is the lamp's quickest control | on |
| `--early-end-ms` | 100–1000 ms with `kept-start`; off. Sends the end early after that much quiet, plays only when sure | — | — | commit af9ff59c5 (direct, 14 Oct) | Played on lamp-4ace at 200 ms: worse, so switched off (the Second's work log, row 19) | off | The end-of-turn model (`--endpoint smart-turn-fast`) took its place | off |
| `--call-wait-ms` | milliseconds; off. An utterance that is only the lamp's name ("Hey Lamp,") waits that long for the rest instead of ending; if nothing more comes, the model is told it was only the name (both routes; on the second, a lone name with no words back is asked as `Lamp.`, #1712) | — (lamp-4ace's drop-in passes 2000) | 2000 (#1332) | #891 | Runs on lamp-4ace with 2000 through 17 Oct. On #828 at 13:03 it was cleared as the cut-in regression's cause, 6 of 10 yields with the wait on against 7 of 10 off; at 12:4x, on five cut-ins, the yields were slower with it on (1.6–2.0 s against 0.5–0.9 s) | 2000 (#1332) | A person who says the name and pauses is waited for, not answered with "Yes?" | on |

## The other parts: body, eyes, expression, tasks and controls

Body flags all say "keep as is until #1192's guard is on the lamp": on 17 Oct a
body exit wrote a bad elbow goal on lamp-4ace, #1192 now refuses such goals, and
its lamp-4ace run is Monday's, with a person there.

| Switch | Values and default | lamp-4ace (repo unit) | Product unit | Added by | Lamp check it named | Proposed product default | Why (one line) | Counted as |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LAMP_UNPLUG | `on` only; off. On Body, Expression, Skills and Voice (which passes it to its ring worker only): guarded latched fold with fresh physical and rendered completion; core tool hidden while off; only a matching "never mind" cancel or a reboot clears an accepted request; with no stand within 60 s of the cancel it fails and says it needs checking (unplug.md) | — | — | #1674 | Host simulations only; physical fold, blocked arm, restart, privacy, visible asleep and spoken completion still need a lamp | off until the full unplug-preparation trial passes | Requires real folded-state, light and speech proof; no lamp evidence yet | until |
| LAMP_UNPLUG_STAY_MIN | whole minutes over zero; 10. On Skills, only with `LAMP_UNPLUG=on`: unbroken minutes Ready before the lamp says it is still plugged in and gets back up, through the same cancel as a spoken "never mind" (unplug.md) | — | — | #2069 | Host tests only; a timeout at 1 min and a hand on the arm after a never mind still need a lamp | 10, inside the opt-in feature | A lamp that stays folded all day because nobody came is the odd behaviour, not the timeout (the first builder, 04:45) | until |
| `lamp-body --idle` | `on`/`off`; on. A lamp standing at rest shifts a little, every 40 s to 3 min at random | on | on | commit 44d8fea57 (direct; as the crate `motor`) | Ran on lamp-4ace (every 18 s before the random waits); body.md counts it as passing its minute, shown | on (already); keep as is until #1192's guard is on the lamp | Folded it never shifts, and since 16 Oct the waits are random, so it reads as alive, not on a clock (body.md "With --idle on") | on |
| `lamp-body --wake` | `on`/`off`; on. Brings a joint found off home back slowly, never away from home | on | on | commit ce40ff5ce (direct) | Shown on lamp-52e6 (base woke 311 and 283 counts); lamp-4ace's own pressed-head case not tried | on (already); keep as is until #1192's guard is on the lamp | Without it a lamp found off home refuses to start and has no body (body.md "Waking") | on |
| `lamp-body --caller-caps` | `on`/`off`; off. The kernel names who sends each datagram: a gesture is held to its unit's most priority, a sight taken from the eyes, skills or root only | — | — | #3504 phase 0, piece 3 | none yet; one with an arm, after #3716 | off until a lamp run | A skill or a login user can claim the person's priority today (body.md "Who asks, from the kernel") | off |

A skill's or idle gesture playing as the lamp starts listening pauses, eased to a hold; it ends when the lamp answers or after 3 s, and goes on where it was for a line left alone | — | — | #2736 | One lamp run with someone at the arm: a skill's gesture, then a line to the lamp mid-gesture; it pauses, resumes only after a hold under 3 s, ends if the lamp answers; the person says whether it reads natural | on after that run (the first builder, 22 Oct) | A creature stops mid-stretch to hear and carries on if nothing was for it | off |
| `lamp-body` LAMP_BODY_OWN_RANGES | `on`; off. Reads each servo's own stored limits at start; a home, stand or pitch window outside them, or a folded arm more than 80 outside them, is refused, and a head or base goal outside them (less 20) ends the run on `Stop::Window`, unless it takes a joint lying past that window back toward it from where it lies, inside the range itself (#3504); a hold where a joint already is (take hold, rest, settle, stop pushing) is checked against the range itself. A joint storing 0–4095 keeps today's guard | — | — | #3558 | lamp-52e6 then lamp-4ace: the body starts and runs a session with it on; the report's `own_ranges` matches each servo's limits; nothing refused | on after those runs (the first builder, #3558) | Each lamp is bounded by its own calibrated range, as the 100 V1 lamps are | until |
| `lamp-eyes --full-look` | `WxH` with `--full-look-file`; off. One larger still when asked, after the head settles | — | `1280x960` with `--full-look-file /run/lampos-eyes/look.jpg` (#2791's product half) | #842 | Works end to end on lamp-4ace, answering since 16 Oct 03:17 (#842); held objects shown on lamp-52e6 | on, with LAMP_LOOK_FULL in the voice's units | Shown on both lamps; the two go on together | now |

Both require a valid separate Store key at `/etc/lampOS/skills/store-key.pub`, placed by factory step 3 with `--store-key FILE`, with no fallback to the update key. Placing the key does not enable the switch. The helper drop-in prepares `PrivateNetwork=no` and IPv4/IPv6 even while off, but its code creates no HTTP client and refuses installs until switch and key checks pass; remove, off and on still run. Answers the app's `skills.install` with a Skills Store catalogue entry (`{"url", "sha256", "name", "version"}`, all but `url` optional): root's helper fetches it over HTTPS (redirects only to https, at most 18 MiB, within 25 s), checks the entry's SHA-256 and `lamp-skill.sig` against the Store key before parsing the manifest, checks paths, kinds, bounds and hashes, then renames it into place, never core, over core, or older than the installed version. `lamp-cloud` fetches and writes nothing. Without the switch or valid key, `not on this lamp: skills.install` (#1722, `cloud.md`, "Installing from the Skills Store"; `skills.md`, "From the Skills Store"; `install.md`, "The Store, prepared but off") | — | — | #1983 (#1722) | Tests only (a signed package installs and is listed; unsigned, another key's, a changed manifest or file, a wrong SHA-256 refused with the folder read-only; over-size, `..`, absolute, hidden, a link, a core name and a package saying core refused; `http://`, `file://`, a user and a redirect to http refused unfetched; the real helper binary refuses on its own, and refuses every install with the switch off in its environment while a remove runs; an older version refused, the same and a newer one taken; the signature check mutation-checked); none named on a lamp yet: an install through the broker on one lamp, its time there, and the helper unit's network | off until its lamp check, and until the Store's server and key | The app's Add button is built against it | until |

A failure with no join not acked within 5 s makes the MQTT link again. A cut change is restored by the unit's `ExecStopPost=` (within 90 s, `TimeoutStopSec=2min`) and, after a power cut, by `lamp-setup-wifi-restore.service` at boot (#1959's P1). The reply is `update.status`'s object with `"check": "started"`; refused with `a round is running` (`now` not `idle` or `unknown`), `asked less than a minute ago`, or `lamp-update is not listening for a check` (no running lamp-update reads checks: an older one, a dry `serve`, or none); `serve` also drops a check that came during a round and takes at most one a minute. ## In plain words

What a person would notice with each switch on, one plain line per switch counted `until` or `now`. The stable notes' "Built but not on yet" list prints these lines instead of the rows' own words, which go under "For developers" there (`lampos-promote`'s notes, the first builder on #3976). A switch the notes list needs a line here (`on_this_repository_each_listed_switch_has_a_plain_line`): one sentence of at most 110 characters, with no switch names, flags or code. A switch that changes nothing a person would notice says so.

| Switch | What a person would notice |
|---|---|
| `LAMP_ADDRESSED` | The lamp checks whether each request was said to it from close by. |
| `LAMP_APP_SCHEDULE` | The app lists the timers and reminders you set by voice, and can cancel them. |
| `LAMP_ATTENTION_NEAR_CALIBRATED` | In a busy room, the lamp knows how loud you sound from your own chair, measured at setup. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_SOCKET` | Nothing you'd notice: opens the speech connection early to try to start talking sooner. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_SOCKET_MODEL` | Nothing you'd notice: picks the voice engine for the early speech connection, keeping one voice per answer. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_KEEPS_LISTENING` | The lamp still stops if you keep talking over it, even after two earlier pauses came to nothing. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_COUNTS_REAL` | Two side remarks while the lamp talks no longer stop it hearing a third line said to it. |
| `LAMP_CUT_IN_NEAR_ASKER` | While answering you, the lamp no longer stops for quieter talk from farther back in the room. |
| `LAMP_BACKCHANNEL_HOLDS` | A short "mm-hm" or "yeah" while the lamp talks no longer stops its answer; it keeps going. |
| `LAMP_BACKCHANNEL_DEBUG` | Nothing you'd notice: for debugging, it logs short replies like "yeah" said while the lamp talks. |
| `LAMP_ASIDES_HOLD` | Short asides like "wow" or "hmm, interesting" while the lamp talks no longer stop its story. |
| `LAMP_CLOUD_TLS` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's connection to the app's server is encrypted. |
| `LAMP_SAID_AS_HEARD` | When you cut the lamp off, it remembers only what it said aloud, so "go on" picks up from there. |
| `LAMP_CUT_INS_NOT_TALK` | Lines said over the lamp's answer and left unanswered no longer count toward a busy room. |
| `LAMP_FACE_GAP_HOLD` | If you turn away briefly mid-chat, the lamp keeps the conversation as yours for up to 12 seconds. |
| `LAMP_FAINT_FLOOR_MAX_DB` | The level below which the lamp ignores faint sound can adjust to the room, up to a set limit. |
| `LAMP_FOLLOWS_ON` | Outside a busy room, a line said within 20 seconds of the lamp finishing can be taken as a follow-up. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_NOT_OWN` | Other people's talk after the lamp answers you no longer keeps the lamp joining in, line after line. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_ASKS` | In a busy room, the lamp takes up to 2 seconds to decide if an unnamed follow-up is meant for it. |
| `LAMP_GOING_ON_SAME_ONE` | In a busy room, unnamed follow-ups are ignored unless they answer the lamp or come in the same voice. |
| `LAMP_NAME_OR_FOLLOW_UP` | In a busy room, facing the lamp isn't enough: say its name, unless you follow up within 8 seconds. |
| `LAMP_HOLDING_ANSWER` | If the lamp only says a short "let me check", it comes back once with the real answer. |
| `LAMP_LISTEN_GAPS` | The lamp leaves a short pause between sentences so a "stop" said over it can be heard. |
| `LAMP_LIVE_SCOPED_SESSION` | When another known person speaks, the lamp starts a fresh chat for them, though their first line may be lost. |
| `LAMP_MISHEARD_HUM` | If the lamp mishears you cutting in, it gives a quick "hm?" and listens instead of asking "Sorry?". |
| `LAMP_NAME_NOTE_HEARD_AS` | The lamp knows its name can sound like "Mom", "man" or "ma'am", so such calls may still reach it. |
| `LAMP_OTHERS_ANSWER` | A "yes" said over the lamp's answer, not to its question, is taken as someone else's and not answered. |
| `LAMP_OUTPUT_CAPS` | Night-time limits on the light and volume stay in force, even after a restart. |
| `LAMP_REACH_PROBE` | When only the lamp's service is down but the internet works, the lamp no longer says it's offline. |
| `--reach-idle` | The lamp notices a lost internet connection within about 30 seconds, even when nobody is talking. |
| `LAMP_REMARKS` | In a busy room, the lamp ignores remarks to others like "She said it might rain" that ask it nothing. |
| `LAMP_RESEND_LOST_LINE` | If the connection drops just after you speak, the lamp sends your request once more instead of losing it. |
| `LAMP_ROOM_FILE` | After a quick restart, the lamp remembers whether the room was quiet or busy instead of starting unsure. |
| `LAMP_SERVICE_DOWN_LINE` | When the lamp's service is down but the internet works, the lamp says so once instead of going silent. |
| `LAMP_PRIVACY_GAP_RIDE` | A split-second glitch reading the privacy switch no longer ends your conversation. |
| `LAMP_NIGHT_ASLEEP_FACE` | Asleep at night with the light off, the lamp shows the same soft warm glow as when you put it to sleep. |
| `LAMP_REMINDER_PULSE` | While the microphones are off, the lamp's light pulses softly once a minute when a reminder is due. |
| `LAMP_BODY_ASK` | When the lamp's arm needs a hand, the lamp says so aloud once, and again when you call its name. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_REFUSED_HOLD` | When the voice service refuses the lamp, it says so once and retries each minute instead of going silent. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH` | Everything the lamp says, reminders and skills included, comes in one chosen voice. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_GEMINI_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address one of the speech services is reached through. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_DEEPSEEK_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sends the lamp's chat through the company's server, so no separate key is needed. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SPEECH_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address the lamp's speech goes through. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LISTENING_BASE_URL` | Nothing you'd notice: sets the server address used to turn your speech into words. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_KEY_TERMS` | The lamp listens out for chosen words, like a city name, so it hears them right. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FIRST_WORDS` | The lamp can start speaking an answer after its first few words, rather than waiting for a full phrase. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FLUX` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp tries a newer service for turning your English speech into words. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FACE_NAME` | When the lamp knows your face, it knows your name too and can say it back when you ask. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SAY_WITH_CALL` | When you ask it to change the light or set a timer, the lamp answers about a second sooner. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_MEMORY_HITS` | The lamp checks what it remembers against each thing you say, so its answers can use those memories. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_QUIET_HELD` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp holds back silence after you pause instead of sending it off. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_PREPARED` | The lamp starts working on its answer as you finish speaking, so it can reply sooner; nothing is said early. |
| `LAMP_NAME_SAID_TO_A_PERSON` | The lamp no longer takes "Ma'am," or "Dad," at a line's start as its name, unless you face or talk with it. |
| `LAMP_NAME_START_USUAL` | Nothing you'd notice: makes sure the lamp's name said first in a line is still caught as a call. |
| `LAMP_NAME_REPEAT_NOT_A_CALL` | A desk remark about the lamp that the transcript repeats is no longer taken as calling it. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_FEW_TOOLS` | Nothing you'd notice: answers start with fewer abilities loaded; remembering takes one extra step. |
| `LAMP_NAMED_ALWAYS` | In a busy room, the lamp answers anyone who starts with "Lamp," while someone faces it. |
| `LAMP_SAME_SPEAKER_CARRIES` | In a busy room, the lamp keeps talking with the same person by face and voice, and lets others' lines pass. |
| `LAMP_BUSY_CALLED_STRICT` | In a busy room with nobody facing it, the lamp ignores words that only sound like its name, like "Mom". |
| `LAMP_REMARK_ABOUT_IT` | In a busy room, the lamp stays quiet when people talk about it, like "that lamp is cute," rather than to it. |
| `LAMP_REMARK_GLANCE` | When people talk about the lamp in a busy room, it gives them one quick glance, at most once a minute. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SKILL_LOOK` | When a skill asks it to look for something, the lamp says "Let me look." and looks right away. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LOOK_NOTE` | While searching for something, the lamp is prompted to try the next spot if a look found nothing. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_LOOK_WALK` | While searching for something you asked for, the lamp checks up to five spots itself, then kindly gives up. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_WRITTEN_WAIT` | The lamp waits up to 7 seconds, not 3, to start its own news or reminders instead of dropping them. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_KNOWN_NAMES` | The lamp better hears the name given at setup and the helpers it hands work to, so they're misheard less. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_CUE_AFTER_MS` | The lamp's little waiting sound can come later, so it doesn't "hmm" just before an answer begins. |
| `LAMP_QUIET_GOING_ON_SAME_ONE` | In a quiet room mid-conversation, the lamp ignores a line it can tell, by voice or face, is someone else's. |
| `LAMP_USE_SKILL` | The lamp can use all your installed skills, even past the point where extra skills would stop working. |
| `LAMP_CONTROLS_WAIT` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp does less busy work while idle, and touches and presses still stop it at once. |
| `LAMP_CAPTURE_WAIT` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp's microphone handling does less busy work while it waits for sound. |
| `LAMP_STOP_HEARD` | A dedicated listener for "Stop" pauses the lamp's reply the moment you say it. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_HOLD` | The lamp no longer stops itself when it says the word "stop" in its own reply. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_KEYTERM` | The lamp listens harder for stop words like "Stop," "Wait" and "Hold on" while it speaks. |
| `LAMP_STOP_WORDS_GAIN_DB` | The lamp turns up what it hears of your voice while it speaks, to catch a quiet "Stop" better. |
| `LAMP_TRANSCRIPTION_LANGUAGE` | Nothing you'd notice: tells the lamp which language you speak, to help it write down what it hears. |
| `LAMP_TURNED_AWAY` | In a busy room mid-conversation, the lamp ignores lines meant for others, like "Mom" or "pass me that". |
| `LAMP_TURNED_SPEAKING` | In a busy room, someone facing the lamp but not speaking no longer counts as talking to it. |
| `LAMP_TURNED_FACE_SPEAKS` | Someone facing the lamp but silent while others talk isn't taken as talking to it; the room counts as busy. |
| `LAMP_TWO_FACES_HELD` | A second face seen just once, like a passer-by or a photo, no longer makes the lamp treat the room as busy. |
| `LAMP_WORKER_SCHEDULING` | Nothing you'd notice: shares the lamp's processor more fairly between its voice and its other parts. |
| `LAMP_UNCLEAR_HUM` | When the lamp can't make out what you said, it gives a quick "hm?" and a listening look instead of silence. |
| `LAMP_VAD_INPUT` | The lamp listens differently for your voice while it talks, to catch a "Stop" said over it. |
| `LAMP_OWN_LINE_KEEPS` | Room noise no longer cancels the lamp's own hellos, reminders or news before they start; its name still does. |
| `LAMP_OWN_LINE_OVER_TALK` | In a busy room, chatter no longer stops the lamp's own news or reminders; its name or a tap still does. |
| `LAMP_MOTION_KEEPS_ANSWER` | The sound of the lamp's own motors while it thinks no longer interrupts its answer. |
| `LAMP_MOTION_VOICE` | The lamp tries to tell your voice from its motor noise when you speak while it moves. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_CALLER_ONLY` | The lamp learns a conversation's voice only from the person who called it, so others' lines don't take over. |
| `LAMP_SAME_VOICE_MODEL` | The lamp checks a line is in the voice of the person it's talking with; another voice ends the chat. |
| `LAMP_VOICE_TONE` | The lamp senses how calm, excited, glad or down you sound, keeping only numbers, never your words or voice. |
| `LAMP_NO_WORDS_AGAIN` | When you call the lamp by name and it comes up empty, it tries again or asks you to repeat, never silent. |
| `LAMP_REQUESTED_AUDIO` | When you ask for a sound by voice, the lamp can play one of its own checked recordings after confirming. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_SEEMS` | When it sees you smiling, the lamp answers in a warmer tone, without ever mentioning how you look. |
| `LAMP_HEALTH_FACTS` | Asked how it is, the lamp can say which of its parts aren't working, and keeps quiet about it otherwise. |
| `LAMP_LIGHT_KEEP` | After a restart, the lamp comes back with the light you last set instead of a default level. |
| `LAMP_SKILL_LIGHT_SPEAKING` | While the lamp speaks, the ring takes on the colour of a light a skill has set, like a warm welcome-back glow. |
| `LAMP_LIGHT_WHILE_MUTED` | You can still change the light from the app while the lamp is muted or its privacy switch is closed. |
| `LAMP_LOOK_FULL_SIDE` | Nothing you'd notice: lets the lamp take larger still pictures, meant for reading handwriting. |
| `LAMP_MAKE_SKILL` | You can ask the lamp to have your computer's agent make a new skill, and it asks before putting it on. |
| `LAMP_APP_MAKE_SKILL` | You can ask for a new skill in the app; the lamp tells you when it's made, and only a spoken yes adds it. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_EARLY_WORDS` | If the lamp recognises you partway through a talk, what you said earlier in it is remembered as yours. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FOLLOWS_FACE` | When you save your face mid-conversation, what you said just before is remembered as yours, not the room's. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_STABLE_ID` | If you change your name with the lamp, it keeps everything it remembered about you under the new name. |
| `LAMP_FORGET_ASKED` | The lamp forgets things only when you ask it to, and checks with you before forgetting everything. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_ABOUT` | Asking the lamp to forget what you said about something forgets just those things and nothing else. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_LINK_USED` | Forgetting one thing no longer takes unrelated talk from the same conversation with it. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_RECALL_V2` | You can ask the lamp what you talked about on a certain day or last week, and its recall is more precise. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_KEEP_HEARD` | When you ask the lamp to remember something, it keeps your own words rather than its rewording. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_REPLACES` | When you correct something the lamp remembered, it keeps the new version in place of the old one. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_REOPEN_QUIET` | After a quiet spell of ten minutes or more, the lamp can follow up on something you told it earlier. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_AFTER_FORGET` | After you say forget everything, the lamp holds back shared notes about people from you for the day. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_ROOM_SPEAKER` | When you say forget everything, the lamp also forgets what it saw you say to the whole room. |
| `LAMP_SECOND_REMEMBER_ASKED` | Asking the lamp to remember your face while giving your name saves them straight away. |
| `LAMP_ONBOARDING_NUDGE` | During setup, the lamp moves through the steps more reliably, catching your name and where you sit. |
| `LAMP_FACE_EXPRESSIONS_ASK` | During setup, the lamp asks once if it may notice whether people look happy or upset, to answer more warmly. |
| `LAMP_PLACE` | Set for one lamp, it knows the town it's in, so weather and nearby questions are answered for there. |
| `LAMP_PERSON_NAME_OWNER_ONLY` | The lamp uses the owner's name only when it sees the owner alone, so visitors aren't called by it. |
| `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` | The lamp skips a request it judges was meant for someone else; changes may take up to 2 seconds longer. |
| `LAMP_FOLLOWS_RUN` | Right after the lamp speaks, a follow-up without its name can still change the light or the volume. |
| `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` | While a longer task runs, the lamp may say a short sentence about how it's going. |
| `LAMP_PROVIDER_WRITES` | Nothing you'd notice: keeps the voice from dropping on a slow network, though answers may come a bit later. |
| `LAMP_SHORT_DONE` | After you ask it to change the light or volume, the lamp answers in a word or two, like Brighter. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_ACCOUNTS` | Questions about your calendar or email go to your computer's agent instead of the lamp's own reminders. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_QUESTIONS` | A question about one of your computer's agents is passed to that agent to answer, not guessed by the lamp. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_SIGN_IN` | You can sign the lamp in to your computer agents by voice: it reads out a code to type and checks your email. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_MACHINE_NAMES` | The lamp calls your computers by their own names when telling you which one has your task. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_NAMED_ASK` | Asking one of your computer's agents by name to do something hands the task straight to that agent. |
| `LAMP_TASKS_CORRECT` | After the lamp reads back a task, you can say no, I said, and correct it before saying yes. |
| `LAMP_UNPLUG` | You can ask the lamp to fold up for unplugging; it says when it's done, and "never mind" undoes it. |
| `LAMP_UNPLUG_STAY_MIN` | If nobody unplugs the folded lamp within 10 minutes, it says it's still plugged in and gets back up. |
| `--overload` | The arm's motors let go when straining hard for a while, then take hold again two minutes later. |
| `LAMP_BODY_OWN_RANGES` | The arm stays within its own calibrated limits and won't move a joint past them. |
| `--steady-rate` | The lamp checks a still face half as often, saving effort but correcting its gaze a little slower. |
| `--look-on-change` | Nothing you'd notice: with nobody around, the camera skips face checks while the view hasn't changed. |
| `--change-persist` | Nothing you'd notice: a brief flicker in a dark room no longer counts as a change, saving more effort. |
| `--leave-still-faces` | The lamp ignores small, faint face-like shapes that never move, which are not real people. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_DROP_OUTLIER` | The lamp still notices who is talking when one camera frame of their face is misread. |
| `LAMP_EYES_SPEAKING_ALL_FACES` | The lamp can tell whether other faces in view are talking, not only the nearest one. |
| `LAMP_TURN_TO_ANSWERED` | As it answers, the lamp turns its head to the one person it saw talking just before. |
| `--visits` | The lamp keeps a rough sense of how often remembered people visit, never when, and tells them it does. |
| `--photo-dir` | Someone can add a face from a photo; the lamp keeps it unconfirmed until that person says yes at the lamp. |
| `LAMP_PHOTO_FACES` | When a person added from a photo is at the lamp, it asks them once whether it may remember them. |
| `LAMP_REMEMBER_SPEAKER` | When asked to remember someone, the lamp learns only the face of the person who introduced themselves. |
| `LAMP_PHOTO_SPEAKER` | The lamp keeps a photo-added face only if the yes comes from that same person, not someone in their place. |
| `LAMP_FACES_PAGE` | The app lists the faces the lamp knows and can remove a person, along with what the lamp remembers of them. |
| `LAMP_MEMORY_FORGET_SOCKET` | Removing a person in the app also erases the lamp's memories of them, without the app ever reading them. |
| `LAMP_SEEMS_BROW_LOW` | The lamp reads lowered brows without a smile as someone feeling low, catching long faces it misses now. |
| `LAMP_FACE_FULL_CROP` | The lamp recognises remembered faces more steadily and keeps their name through a brief miss. |
| `LAMP_FACE_KNOWN_HOLD` | The lamp keeps a remembered face's name through a brief miss, without changing how it reads faces. |
| `--seems-log` | Nothing you'd notice: keeps a short-lived log of expression reads, never whose face, to measure accuracy. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp tracks whether anyone is at the desk, for features like the light going out. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE_VOICE` | Chatter near the lamp no longer counts as someone at the desk; only speech said to the lamp does. |
| `LAMP_PRESENCE_FAR` | A still face far from the lamp, like someone behind or a picture, no longer counts as someone at the desk. |
| `LAMP_WHO_WAS_HERE_KNOWN` | Asking who was here can tell you that someone the lamp knows came by, without saying who. |
| `LAMP_WHO_WAS_HERE_NAMES` | Asking who was here names visitors who agreed to be named; others stay unnamed. |
| `LAMP_WINDOW_FROM_START` | A follow-up begun soon after the lamp spoke is answered, however long it goes on. |
| `LAMP_EYES_YAWN` | The lamp counts your yawns while you sit, only with your okay for face expressions, keeping just the count. |
| `--mouth-on-heard` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp studies mouths closely only while it hears speech, saving effort. |
| `--mouth-runtime` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp reads mouth shapes with a different, faster engine. |
| `--not-while-speaking` | The lamp no longer mistakes its own voice for someone calling its name while it talks. |
| `--quiet-below` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp rests its name listener in a silent room, saving effort, and hears the same. |
| `--rescue-model` | On test lamps only: a second listener helps the lamp hear its name more often. |
| `--stop-least` | Nothing you'd notice: the lamp also listens for the word stop, ready for a feature that uses it. |
| `--offline-face` | When the lamp is offline, its light no longer shows it's ready to listen. |
| `--set-up-later` | Handing work to your computers can be set up after install, and the lamp says why it's off until then. |
| `--targets-from-machines` | Once signed in, the lamp learns your computers by itself so it can hand work to them. |
| `--sign-in-by-code` | You can sign the lamp in by voice, with a code it reads out, so it can hand work to your computers. |
| `--folder-missing` | If handed-off work can't start because its folder isn't on that computer, the lamp says so plainly. |
| `--engines-ready` | Before handing off work, the lamp checks the computer's assistant is signed in and says if it isn't. |
| `--route` | Work you hand off goes into the session on your computer it belongs to, not a new one each time. |
| `LAMP_CONTROLS_STRIP` | The touch strip on the lamp's base responds to touches and strokes. |
| `LAMP_SLEEP_LINE` | Holding the lamp's head to put it to sleep, it says how to wake it before it stops listening. |
| `LAMP_HEAD_PET` | Petting the lamp's head gets a response and a little wiggle instead of putting it to sleep. |
| `LAMP_EARS_CLASSES` | Nothing you'd notice: with your okay, the lamp tells speech, music and laughter apart, keeping no audio. |
| `LAMP_MOOD` | The lamp has a mood: its light and head breathe quicker when lively and slower when dozing. |
| `LAMP_MOOD_TONE` | How your words sound nudges the lamp's mood, seen only in its breathing and liveliness; nothing is kept. |
| `LAMP_MESSAGES` | You can text the lamp from the app and it texts back, and it can send you news of tasks unasked. |
| `LAMP_SKILLS_STORE` | You can install skills from the Skills Store with the app's Add button, each checked before it goes on. |
| `LAMP_SKILLS_SHARE` | You can send a skill you made to the Skills Store; a reviewer reads it before others can add it. |
| `LAMP_APP_RESTART` | You can restart the lamp from the app; it waits for a pause in talk and never restarts during an update. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_FROM_APP` | You can turn guard mode on and off from the app, not only by voice. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_SOUNDS` | In guard mode, a door or footsteps followed by a face or speech counts as someone arriving. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_NAMES` | Guard mode's texts name a visitor the lamp knows if they agreed to be named. |
| `LAMP_GUARD_WORDS_V2` | Saying you're back or that it can stop watching turns guard mode off, instead of on. |
| `LAMP_REST_THROUGH_SOUNDS` | At night, room sounds without words no longer keep the lamp awake; words, its name or a touch still do. |
| `LAMP_YAWN_SOONER` | Once the lamp has seen you yawn, your stretch break comes at 30 minutes instead of 50. |
| `LAMP_SETTINGS_WIFI` | You can change the lamp's Wi-Fi from the app; if the new one fails, it goes back to the old one. |
| `LAMP_WIFI_RECOVERY` | If the lamp can't reach its Wi-Fi, it opens setup and asks you to set its Wi-Fi again in the app. |
| `LAMP_OWNER_PLACE` | The app can give the lamp your rough area, so "near me" and weather questions are answered for it. |
| `LAMP_CONTROL` | Nothing you'd notice: lets paired computers on your network control the lamp, for developers. |
| `LAMP_CONTROL_RATES` | Nothing you'd notice: for developers, programs may ask the lamp as often as they like; the lamp's body still keeps every move safe. |
| `LAMP_SHUTDOWN` | You can turn the lamp off from the app; it rests, shows a safe-to-unplug light and powers down. |

## What the census found

Gaps between the docs, the units and the code, for whoever owns each:

- **lamp-4ace's repo unit sets less than the docs say lamp-4ace runs.**
  `docs/tasks.md` says `LAMP_TASKS_WHO` and `LAMP_TASKS_ASKED` stay on on
  lamp-4ace (15 Oct 21:30), `docs/attention.md` records
  `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` taken there on the merged build (16 Oct 07:25),
  #997 and #1148 describe head taps (`LAMP_HEAD_TOUCH`) and the 12:20 mute
  (`--controls-mute`), #842 the full look (LAMP_LOOK_FULL with `lamp-eyes
  --full-look`, since 16 Oct 03:17), #1242 `LAMP_START_OFFLINE`, and
  second-voice.md the backup voice's config.
  `deploy/lamp-4ace/lamp-voice.service` sets none of them,
  so they live in drop-ins on the lamp that the repo doesn't have. Before
  switching groups on, the lamp's own drop-ins should be read and copied here.
- **No repo policy unit passes `--act attention` or `--act progress`; the
  product units pass `--hello --act hello --gate --hello-by-rule`** (the
  first builder, 30 Oct, on the launch testers' findings and on #4625), so
  Welcome back's hello, asked of the gate even with `LAMP_SKILLS_ASK=skip`,
  is decided by the hard rules alone, one hello an hour to each person
  among them, at once; the judge is asked only in shadow and never decides
  (skills.md, policy.md). From
  the repo units alone `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` does nothing, and `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS` only
  makes what it holds 2 s late: it waits for a decision that never comes,
  then goes on (`policy_holds.rs`).
- **Removed (#2825):** `LAMP_SPEAK_FIRST` and `LAMP_HELLO_ASKS_POLICY` went
  with the voice's own hello; the hello on an arrival is the Welcome back
  skill's alone ([speaking-first.md](speaking-first.md#one-hello-from-the-skill-2825)).
  A unit that still sets either changes nothing.
- **lamp-tasks:** the product unit passes `--progress --push-wait`; the
  lamp-4ace unit doesn't (reverted in `4b8922bbc`, its build being older than
  `--push-wait`), while `docs/tasks.md` says lamp-4ace runs the same. Both
  units pass `--watch`, which changes nothing.
- **Head touch:** the #2948 carry pairs product Controls' `--touch`, Voice's
  `LAMP_HEAD_TOUCH`, `--controls-mute` and `LAMP_RESTORED_MUTE`, Expression's
  `--head-touch`, and pins without `legacy_head`. Install preflights these
  product units and relevant preserved overrides after its updater lock,
  before state handling, users, links, units or head files change. It uses
  the updater's literal-mode rules and refuses conflicting or unknown
  modes without rewriting local drop-ins or head files. The attended hand
  and injected-input recovery evidence above are separate. The cue default
  is -20 dBFS after the c0a4 tap-hum comparison and First's 11:40 approval
  (#3275); that ear check does not qualify every path sharing the default.
- **`LAMP_SHORT_SOUNDS=quiet-room`** is in both units, the form #800 showed
  answering a room sound; `quiet-room-checked`, which fixes it, was in neither
  until #1332 put it in the product unit.
- **Stale text:** `deploy/lamp-4ace/RUNBOOK.md` says `LAMP_ROOM_FACTS` is "off;
  not yet heard" while the unit sets it on; the "Switch" section of
  `docs/attention.md` says `busy-near` isn't on lamp-4ace while the same file
  records it on since 14 Oct 18:46; `docs/tasks.md`'s "Not shown yet" for
  #903 (`handovers.sh` ran on lamp-4ace on 16 Oct) and its "Nothing was run
  on a lamp" for #769 (its 15 Oct 17:47 minute); test comments in `lamp-body.rs` say
  `--wake`, `--rest-glance` and `--look-at-name` are off unless asked for, while
  the code defaults them on; the units' comments say the Policy's gate "is not
  built yet (#939)" while `docs/skills.md` describes it built (#958).
- **Five of the voice's own flags default in code to what no lamp runs** (`--turn-taking`, `--endpoint`, `--noise-suppression`, `--reference-clock` and `--provider-continuation`): both units pass the lamp's choice, so a lamp started without the unit's line gets the experiment's first form.
- **A product body's motion flags are partly per lamp:** `BODY_POSES` in a
  lamp's `/etc/lampOS/body/lamp.env` can carry flags, and lamp-06c1's passes
  `--torque on`.
- **Small ones:** `LAMP_SCHEDULE_SPEAK` is defined twice (`reminders.rs` and
  `tools.rs`); `LAMP_ONBOARDING` has no doc comment; #987
  (`LAMP_LOOK_FULL_SIDE`) names no lamp check.
- **`LAMP_SCHEDULE_SPEAK=skill` never speaks a reminder** (the reviewer on
  #1238, a probe test on #1038, 08:28; the Second's fix is #1253): the coordinator takes `skill` as
  speaking (`reminders.rs`), but only `on` passes `--schedule-speak` to the
  provider worker (`tools.rs`), so the worker defers every due reminder as
  disabled (`provider_worker.rs`, `reminder_policy.rs`). It came in with
  #1038. Fixed since: the worker gets `--schedule-speak` under `skill` too
  (`tools::tests::schedule_speech_by_skill_gives_the_worker_its_flag`).
- **`LAMP_KERNEL_CLOCK` never reached the provider worker:** it is spawned
  with `env_clear()` and the switch isn't passed on (`process.rs`), so the
  worker's `the_time` and session note never saw the clock unset (Ten on
  #705, 17 Oct). Fourth passes it in #1158.
- **#1332 brought the product voice unit up to lamp-4ace's** (the first
  builder, 17 October 15:41, for Sunday's reinstall and every product lamp):
  20 switches lamp-4ace ran all through 16 and 17 October, `--reference-recovery
  bounded` (after lamp-cb94's crash, #1298) and `--call-wait-ms 2000`. Their
  "Product unit" cells say "(#1332)". For several of them, this page's
  proposal was "off until" a named lamp check that no doc records yet
  (`LAMP_STAFF_NOTE`, `LAMP_TASKS_WHO`, `LAMP_LIGHT_REQUESTS`,
  `LAMP_FIRST_WORDS` among them). The first builder chose to ship them as
  lamp-4ace runs them, and the proposals stay as written, for each one's
  check. Left out of the product at that review: what needs the second voice's
  configuration, the office's own, head touch and `--controls-mute`.
  **Six more were left out at #1332's review** (Nine's P2, `a9c9153ac`),
  because the product's other parts don't pass the flags they need:
  `LAMP_POLICY_HOLDS_ACTIONS`, `LAMP_HELLO_ASKS_POLICY` (removed since with
  the voice's own hello, #2825) and
  `LAMP_PROGRESS_NEWS` need the policy unit's `--act attention`, `--hello`
  and `--act progress`, which it doesn't pass, so they would only make what
  they hold 2 s late or do nothing; `LAMP_LOOK_FULL` needs the eyes'
  `--full-look` and its file (both in the product since #2791's product half); `LAMP_RESTORED_MUTE` needs `--controls-mute`.
  `LAMP_MARK_WEIRD` is left out because a reset doesn't wipe its marks.

### Product update settings

The existing `LAMP_UPDATE_APPLY` switch is explicitly `on` in the product
updater unit. Explicit `off` still prevents settings writes. When it is
absent on an older product install, automatic settings apply requires an
authenticated level-6 product carrier, `LAMP_UPDATE_SELF=on`, and a regular
base updater service using its managed `update/current` link. Other
installations and older non-product manifests keep the off default. The
settings allowlist, signed helper request and three-way local-edit policy
are unchanged; see [updates](updates.md#product-service-units-and-settings-over-the-air).
